「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる世界
〜中国の「強すぎる供給⼒」がもたらす構造変化〜
【みずほ経済インサイト】

### Page 2

本資料の要旨
1
１．「チャイナショック2.0」とは何か P2～12
１．「チャイナショック2.0」とは何か P2～12
• 「チャイナショック」とは、中国の「強すぎる供給力」と輸出攻勢が世界の産業・貿易構造を変える現象
• 「2.0」の特徴は、労働集約型産業での競争力を維持したまま、技術・資本集約型産業での競争力を高めていること
• 背景にあるのが中国特有の技術革新モデル。産業集積と規模の経済もあり、中国企業の競争力が格段に向上
２．「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる欧州とASEAN P13～24
２．「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる欧州とASEAN P13～24
• 欧州は輸出減少と輸入増加に直面。基幹産業でも中国製品の存在感が高まり、自動車産業を中心に競争が本格化
• ASEANは中国からの中間財調達が輸出産業にとって必須化。ただし、最終財を中心に域内産品との競合は強まる
• 中国製品の浸透を受け、欧州、ASEANとも貿易障壁措置を拡大。中国企業は貿易障壁を意識し、現地生産を志向

３．「チャイナショック2.0」に相対する世界 P25～32
３．「チャイナショック2.0」に相対する世界 P25～32
• 世界は競争と相互依存のジレンマに直面。国内製造業の強化で中国に対抗しつつ、安価な中国製品の輸入も継続
• 中国は、対中規制や追加関税への報復措置をちらつかせつつ、自由貿易体制の維持を図って自国の利益を最大化
• 中国が新興国においてAI経済圏を形成し、世界経済システムを再構築する「チャイナショック3.0」に発展する可能性も
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

2005年 2015年 2024年（％）
「チャイナショック2.0」とは何か――中国の「強すぎる供給力」が世界を変える現象
 中国の「強すぎる供給力」と輸出攻勢が世界の産業・貿易構造を変える「チャイナショック2.0」が進行中
― 中国がWTO加盟を契機に生産・組立拠点として世界貿易体制に組み込まれたのが「チャイナショック1.0」
―「2.0」では、中国企業の競争力と供給力が飛躍的に向上し、先端産業分野で先進国との競合が激化
 「チャイナショック2.0」の特徴は、中国が労働集約型産業での輸出競争力を維持したまま、技術・資本集約型産業での競争
力を高めていること。中国企業は繊維・履物などの軽工業品からハイテク製品まで幅広い分野で輸出を伸ばしている
― 他方、「自立自強」政策による輸入代替の進展と内需の低迷で輸入が停滞。その結果、貿易不均衡が拡大
3
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

「チャイナショック2.0」はなぜ起きたのか――「中国式イノベーション」と製造業の生産性向上
 「チャイナショック2.0」の背景にあるのが「中国式イノベーション」。新興分野で中国の民営企業が躍進
― 「中国式イノベーション」とは、国家の戦略的主導と民間の市場志向が組み合わさった技術革新モデル（筆者定義）
― その土台にあるのが産業集積と規模の経済。こうした環境下、中国企業の技術力とコスト競争力が格段に向上
 中国の製造業は、政府の産業支援も背景に積極的な設備投資を行い、自動化と生産の効率化が急速に進展
― 産業用ロボットの新規導入台数（2025年）は35.4万台と世界（60.3万台）の過半を占め、稼働台数でも群を抜く
― 世界経済フォーラム（WEF）が認定するライトハウス（先進工場）拠点数で中国は最多。特に生産性の評価が高い

4
「中国式イノベーション」の特徴（筆者定義） 産業用ロボット稼働台数（左）とライトハウス拠点数（右）

製造業付加価値は世界の3割弱を占める規模。貿易構造が高度化しつつある様子も
 中国はすでに「世界の工場」の地位を確立。製造業付加価値（2025年）は4.8兆ドルと、世界の3割弱を占める規模
― 中国の輸出額（2025年）は3.77兆ドルと、世界の14.4％を占める。貿易黒字も1.18兆ドルと圧倒的な大きさ
― 輸出総額だけでなく、技術集約的なハイテク製品（航空、コンピューターなど）の輸出額も8,250億ドルと世界一
 加えて、近年は貿易構造が消費財（最終財）中心から中間財や資本財へと高度化しつつある様子もうかがえる
― より付加価値の高い製品の貿易特化係数（輸出入バランスから貿易品目の国際競争力を示す指標）が上昇傾向
5

軽工業品の高い競争力を維持しつつ、技術力が必要な化学や機械でも競争力向上

 中国は、軽工業品の高い競争力を維持しつつ、技術力が必要な化学や機械などでも競争力を向上させている
― HSコード別の貿易特化係数を見ると、2025年は2005年と比較して輸出競争力が大幅に向上している品目が多い
― 他方で、食品・原材料・素材の多くは輸入に頼る。「世界の工場」における需要拡大や食の多様化などが背景に
6

類
似
度
高
中国と先進国との輸出競合度が急上昇。自動車が最大の上昇要因
 中国と先進国の輸出類似度指数（ESI）は「チャイナショック2.0」で急上昇。両者の競合が強まっていることを示唆
― 日本は「1.0」と「2.0」の時期に、EUは2010年代中盤から「2.0」の時期にかけて、ESIが顕著に上昇
― 実際、EUは「2.0」の「震度」が大きく、中国製品の浸透による製造業の衰退が最も懸念されている地域
― 産業別に見ると、「2.0」では日本、EUともに自動車が最大の上昇要因になっていることが分かる
10

①チャイナショック2.0とは何か

### Page 12

「チャイナショック2.0」のもう一つの特徴は輸入の停滞。背景に「自立自強」の進展
 「チャイナショック2.0」のもう一つの特徴は輸入の停滞。「自立自強」による輸入代替（国産化）の進展が背景に
― かつて加工貿易が主流であった頃は、輸出製品を生産するため中間財や資本財の輸入が増え、先進国にも恩恵
― 世界の輸出入に占める中国のシェアを見ると、輸出が「1.0」の頃からほぼ一貫して上昇しているのに対し、輸入は2010
年代に入って頭打ちとなっていることが分かる
― 世界の輸入に占める中国のシェアを品目別に見ても、2024年は多くの業種で2015年からシェアが縮小傾向にある

### Page 22

ASEAN5：国産品・中国産品の調達額変化の比較
ASEAN：一方、中国産品とASEAN域内産品の競合は強まる傾向
21
 素材や消費財、機械・電気機器等では、中国製品がASEAN域内産品を圧迫。最終需要で競合する分野が多い傾向
― 上記分野では中国産品の供給増と域内産品の供給減が同時に発生。中国産品が域内産品を上回って増加する例も
― 中間需要は3分野が該当。一方、最終需要では5分野が該当し、消費財等の部門でより強く競合している可能性を示唆
（注） ADBの国際産業連関表（実質ベース、2010年基準）をASEAN5の国際産業連関表へと加工し、中間投入・最終需要品それぞれにおける国産品・中国産品の金額を求めたもの
（出所） ADBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

22
 EUは中国製品の流入拡大を背景に、貿易措置を積極化
― 対中貿易措置は2023年以降に増加。化学品や鉄鋼など幅広い品目が対象。2024年には中国製EVへの追加関税を発

― 一方、直近ではドイツも保護主義路線へと徐々にシフト。背景には主力産業である自動車業界による路線変更
 ASEANも貿易保護措置を拡大する傾向。鉄鋼製品等へのアンチダンピング・セーフガードのほか、越境ECへも対応を拡大
― ただし、対中貿易措置の比率は目立って高まらず。外交関係への配慮から、中国以外の地域も含む形で規制を強化

「チャイナショック2.0」の光と影――安価な製品供給の恩恵と先進国の競争力喪失リスク
 中国の「強すぎる供給力」は、世界市場に低価格と安定供給という恩恵をもたらしている
― 中国の輸出価格が相対的に低下する一方、輸出数量はハイペースで拡大しており、輸出ドライブが鮮明に
― 輸出ドライブによる安価な中国製品の供給拡大が世界の財価格を押し下げ、インフレ圧力の緩和にも寄与
 他方で、中国の高い輸出競争力は輸入国の産業競争力の低下と対中依存度の上昇を招いていると考えられる

― 先進国が中国企業の技術革新力とコスト競争力に圧倒され、得意としてきた先端産業で競争力を喪失するリスク
― 関税などで対中輸入を抑制しても、供給網を通じた中国依存は容易に低下せず。経済安保上の脆弱性は残存
26

（出所） CPB、LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 28

「チャイナショック2.0」への対応――国内製造業の強化で対抗しつつ、中国製品の輸入も継続
 世界は競争と相互依存のジレンマに直面。国内製造業の強化で中国に対抗しつつ、安価な中国製品の輸入も継続
― 先進国は、戦略分野での産業基盤の強化や対中デリスキングを図りつつ、非機微分野で貿易・投資活動を維持
― 新興国は、脱中国化を図る供給網の一部を取り込んで国内産業の育成を企図。過剰な中国製品の流入には警戒も
 中国は、対中規制や追加関税への報復措置をちらつかせつつ、自由貿易体制の維持を図って自国の利益を最大化
― 過剰生産能力への批判を一蹴し輸出拡大。一方で、関税回避や規制対応のため、海外での現地生産も積極展開
27
「チャイナショック2.0」への対抗と協調

### Page 29

EUは産業競争力の回復に向け、政策ロードマップを策定。IAAにより域内調達も強化へ
 第二期フォンデアライエン体制下で、EUは「競争力回復」に向けた政策のロードマップ（競争力コンパス）を策定
― （１）米中とのイノベーション格差是正、（２）脱炭素化と競争力強化の両立、（３）過剰な域外依存の軽減と安全保障の強
化を目指す。加えて、市場統合の深化や規制緩和等を内容とする（４）分野横断的取り組みを実行する構え
 競争力コンパス枠組みのもと、欧州は原産地規則の強化により、EVや鉄鋼産業等の域内調達を強力に推進する方針
― 2026年3月以降、欧州委員会は域内調達の拡大に向け、IAA（産業加速法）の立法化に着手
― IAAの現行案では、EVの公共調達や補助金等の支援に対し、域内調達・組立の要件を導入予定
（注） 7要件：①実質的な内国民待遇、②経済安全保障、③競争条件＋ILO、④パリ協定＋炭素価格、⑤企業行動・人権、⑥低炭素需要政策、⑦迂回防止
（出所） 欧州委員会（2025）“A Competitiveness Compass for the EU(factsheet)”、ほか各種資料より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 31

政策の概要国
• 2026年9月、輸入電気自動車への物品税引上げを決定。バッテリーEVのうち国内生産がなく、国産部品も使
用していない車両には2027年から物品税を10％→35％に引き上げる計画
• 現地部品の使用など、一定の国内調達や今後の調達率引き上げが見込まれるものについては、税率引き下
げも検討
タイ
• 四輪電気自動車（BEV）について国産化率規定を設置。政府は2026年までに40％、2030年以降に80％と段
階的な基準値引き上げを目指す
• 完成車輸入優遇の利用企業に、2027年末までに輸入台数以上の国内生産を義務付け
インドネシア
• 2026年7月、EVの国内生産・部品製造を支援するEVインセンティブ戦略（EVIS）を導入。交付された納税証
明書を各種納税に充当することで、企業が税務負担を軽減できる仕組み
• 一定の投資・生産要件を満たす企業に対し、投資額の最大40％を支給
• 国内製造・組立車両の生産実績に応じ、工場出荷価格の最大12％相当を支給
フィリピン
• 国内組立EVや関連部品の税金を2027年末まで一部免除（EV本体：物品税・販売税、部品：輸入関税）

• 完成車輸入に車両価格、およびエンジン容量の下限を設定（2026年7月～）マレーシア
• 国内で製造・組立を行い、生産台数等の要件を満たす企業に、国内生産できない自動車部品の輸入関税を
免除。環境対応車も対象ベトナム
ASEANは、対中関係深化のメリットを維持すべく、自動車を中心に現地生産を促す
30
 ASEANは現地生産要件の設置などで、中国との貿易関係や中国企業の投資流入を維持しながら、輸入品との競合による
域内産業への圧力緩和を目指す方向
― 2025年以降、ASEAN5各国は、自動車を中心に現地生産の促進や国内規定を徐々に厳格化
― 輸入車を中心にEVの浸透が進む中、現地生産化の促進により、中国企業による域内投資や地場サプライヤー・雇用へ
の波及効果を得る狙い

おわりに：「チャイナショック2.0」とAI――製造業へのAI実装で一段と高まる中国の競争力
 中国は、AI技術で世界をリード。オープンウェイト戦略と国家主導のガバナンスで独自のAIエコシステムを構築しつつある
― 大規模言語モデルの性能は米国に肉薄。コストパフォーマンスで海外にも普及し、トークン消費量のシェアが急拡大
― 同時に、ヒューマノイドや自動運転といったフィジカルAI分野でも、技術開発と社会実装が急ピッチで進んでいる
 中国は今後、製造業へのAI実装によって生産性を向上、技術革新を加速させ、競争力を一段と高めるとみられる
― 中国政府は、経済社会の幅広い分野でAI導入を図る「AI＋（プラス）」政策を推進。「AI＋製造業」により研究開発設計、
生産、物流、サービスなど各バリューチェーンへの実装が進めば、「強すぎる供給力」がさらに強力に

Document 084: 2026年7-9月期GDP（１次速報値） ～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7-9月期GDP（１次速報値） ～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026年 11月16日

1/5 
2026年 7-9月期 GDP（１次速報値） 
～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

小幅マイナス成長も、景気回復の基調は維持 
本日内閣府から公表された 2026年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率（１次速報）は前期比年率
▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）と、４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった1。個人消費が前期比▲0.3％
と減少したほか、住宅投資や公共投資も減少し、成長率を押し下げている。一方、設備投資は前
期比＋1.6％と高い伸びとなり、景気を下支えした。概ね事前の市場予想（前期比年率＋0.2％、
筆者予想：同▲0.4％）の範囲内であり、サプライズはない。 
今回減少した個人消費については、過度に悲観する必要はないだろう。４-６月期にみられた自
動車やエアコンなどの耐久財需要の一時的な押し上げが一巡したことに加え、７-９月期には台風
や大雨、長雨などの天候不順が相次いだことも下押し要因となったとみられる。一方、名目賃金
の高い伸びを背景に実質賃金の増加が続くなど、家計の所得環境は足元で改善している。食料品
や日用品などを中心とした物価の高止まりが消費の回復を妨げている面はあるものの、今回の減
少をもって個人消費の基調が悪化したと判断する必要はないだろう。 
一方、設備投資が高い伸びとなったことは好材料だ。今回の増加には前期の特殊要因の反動も
含まれているが、それを除いても設備投資は増加したと考えられる。企業収益が高水準を維持す
るなか、人手不足に対応した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は根強い。企業
部門の底堅さが改めて確認されたと評価できる。 
このように、７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰは４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となったが、減少幅は僅か
だ。前期まで３四半期連続で比較的高い成長が続いていたことに加え、今回の個人消費の減少に
は、耐久財需要の剥落や８～９月の天候不順といった一時的な下押し要因が影響したとみられる
点も重要だ。今回のマイナス成長をもって景気回復の流れが途切れたと判断する必要はないだろ
う。家計部門は力強さを欠く一方、設備投資を中心に企業部門は堅調さを維持している。家計と
企業の回復に濃淡はみられるものの、日本経済は均してみれば緩やかな回復基調にあると評価で
きる。

1 ＧＤＰ統計では、季節調整のかけ直しや基礎統計の改定に伴い過去の値も毎回改定される。25年４-６月期が前期比
年率＋0.8％（改定前＋0.5％）、７-９月期が▲1.5％（同▲1.5％）、10-12月期が＋1.1％（同＋1.1％）、26年１-３月期
が＋1.9％（同＋1.9％）、４-６月期が＋1.7％（同＋1.4％）などとなった。符号の変化等はみられず、景気認識に変更
を迫るほどではなかった。

### Page 2

2/5

輸入増による成長率押し下げと公的在庫のプラス寄与は表裏一体 
今回のＧＤＰを評価する際に注意が必要なのは、輸入と公的在庫の動きである。 
４-６月期は、中東情勢の悪化によって原油輸入が激減したことが、ＧＤＰ統計上は輸入減を通
じて成長率を押し上げた。一方、輸入の減少による国内供給の不足を補うために国家備蓄原油が
放出され、公的在庫の取り崩しが進んだ。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.5％Pt（前
期比年率寄与度▲2.0％Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し下げた。輸入減による成長率の押し上げは、
公的在庫の減少と表裏一体の関係にあった。 
７-９月期は、これと逆の動きが生じた。政府・民間による代替調達の進展によって原油輸入が
回復したため、統計上は輸入増が成長率の押し下げ要因となった。一方、輸入の回復に伴って国
家備蓄原油の取り崩しは一服しており、公的在庫の減少幅は前期に比べて大きく縮小した。この
結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.5％Pt（前期比年率寄与度＋2.0％Pt）と、成長率を大き
く押し上げている。 
輸入増による成長率の押し下げ（前期比年率寄与度▲2.2％Pt）と公的在庫変動による押し上げ
（同寄与度＋2.0％Pt）は、ともに原油の供給環境が改善したことに伴う動きである。両者を個別
に景気の強弱として評価することは適切ではなく、一体として見るべきだろう。 
むしろ重要なのは、代替調達の進展によって国家備蓄への依存を減らしながら必要な原油を確
保できる状況になり、資源不足が生産活動を大きく制約する事態が回避されたことである。ＧＤ
Ｐ統計上、輸入の増加は成長率の押し下げ要因となるものの、経済実態としては国内で利用可能
な資源の供給回復を意味している。この点は、７-９月期の日本経済を評価するうえで、むしろ前
向きに捉えるべきだろう。

10-12月期はプラス成長を予想 
 先行きについても、景気は緩やかな回復基調を維持すると予想する。７-９月期は４四半期ぶり
のマイナス成長となったが、耐久財特需の剥落や天候不順による一時的な下押しが和らぐことか
ら、10-12月期には再びプラス成長に戻る可能性が高い。名目賃金の高い伸びや雇用の改善が続く
なか、所得環境の改善が個人消費を下支えするとみられる。 
ただし、今後は物価上昇率の再加速が個人消費の重荷になると予想される。既往の原油高や原
材料費、物流費などの上昇が時間差を伴って川下へ波及しつつある。今後は食料品や日用品など
への価格転嫁がさらに進むことに加え、電気・ガス代も上昇するだろう。物価上昇率の加速によ
り、足元で続いている実質賃金の改善が一服する恐れがある。個人消費は先行き持ち直すとみる
が、回復ペースは力強さを欠くとみられる。 
一方、企業部門については今後も底堅い推移が期待できる。企業収益が高水準を維持するな
か、人手不足に対応した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は根強い。輸入資本
財価格や建設コストの上昇は下押し要因となるものの、設備投資は緩やかな増加基調を続けると
予想する。また、米国を中心とした海外景気の底堅さやグローバルなＡＩ関連需要の拡大も、輸

### Page 3

3/5 
出や生産を下支えするだろう。 
このように、10-12月期は個人消費が緩やかに持ち直すことに加え、設備投資や輸出の増加が景
気を下支えし、再びプラス成長になると予想する。物価高による個人消費への下押しには注意が
必要だが、企業部門の堅調さが維持されることで、日本経済は緩やかな回復を続けるだろう。

需要項目別の詳細 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と２四半期連続で減少した。形態別では、耐久財消費が前期比▲
3.5％（４-６月期：同＋3.6％）、非耐久財消費が同▲0.2％（４-６月期：同▲1.9％）、半耐久財
消費が同▲1.4％（４-６月期：同＋0.4％）、サービス消費が同＋0.1％（４-６月期：同▲0.1％）
となっている。４-６月期には環境性能割の廃止を受けた自動車販売の増加や、27年４月の省エネ
基準強化を控えたエアコンの前倒し購入など、耐久財消費の特需がみられたが、７-９月期にはこ
うした押し上げ効果が一巡した。また、８～９月には記録的な豪雨や台風、長雨などの天候不順
が相次いだことが、外出手控えによる消費機会の減少や旅行・宿泊の見送りなどを通じて下押し
に繋がったとみられる。また、非耐久財消費にも弱さがみられる。食料品や日用品の価格上昇が
続くなか、家計の節約志向が根強いことに加え、天候不順による季節商品の販売減少なども下押
しに効いた可能性がある。 
もっとも、名目賃金の高い伸びを背景に実質賃金の増加が続くなど、所得環境は足元で改善し
ており、家計を取り巻く環境が悪化しているわけではない。食料品を中心とした価格水準の高止
まりが消費の回復を妨げている面はあるものの、４-６月期には高校授業料の実質無償化拡充や学
校給食費無償化に伴う個人消費から政府消費への振り替わりなどの特殊要因があり、今回７-９月
期についても耐久財需要の反動や天候不順といった一時的な下押しが大きく影響したとみられ
る。個人消費が力強さを欠いていることは確かだが、今回の減少をもって消費の基調が悪化した
と判断する必要はないだろう。 
先行きについては、耐久財需要の反動や天候不順といった一時的な下押しが和らぐことに加
え、賃金上昇や雇用の改善が下支えとなることから、個人消費は緩やかに持ち直すと予想する。
ただし、26年度後半には既往の輸入コスト上昇の価格転嫁が進み、食料品や日用品などの値上が
りや電気・ガス代の上昇が家計の実質購買力を圧迫する可能性が高い。物価上昇率の再加速によ
って実質賃金の改善が一服するリスクがあり、消費の回復ペースは当面緩やかなものにとどまる
だろう。 
設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と高い伸びとなり、３四半期ぶりに増加した。４-６月期には特許権
の海外譲渡に伴う研究開発投資の減少という特殊要因により押し下げられていたが、７-９月期に
はその影響が剥落したことが前期比での押し上げ要因となった。もっとも、こうした特殊要因を
除いても設備投資は増加したと考えられる。企業収益が高水準を維持するなか、人手不足に対応
した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は引き続き強い。各種調査でも企業の設
備投資計画は高い伸びを維持しており、設備投資は均してみれば緩やかな増加基調にあると判断
できる。

### Page 4

4/5
先行きについても、輸入資本財価格や建設コストの上昇が設備投資の下押し要因となるもの
の、高水準の企業収益や根強い省力化・デジタル・ＡＩ関連投資需要が下支えとなり、緩やかな
増加基調が続くと予想する。
なお、特許権の海外への譲渡により、テクニカルに４-６月期はサービス輸出が上振れ、設備投
資が下振れたが、７-９月期はその剥落によりサービス輸出が下振れ、設備投資が上振れている。
両者は相殺し合うため、成長率全体でみれば概ねニュートラルとなる。
輸出は前期比▲0.2％と小幅減となった。４-６月期には大口要因によって研究開発サービスの
受取が急増していたが、７-９月期にはその反動によってサービス輸出が前期比▲7.1％（４-６月
期：＋8.9％）と大幅に減少した。一方、財輸出は前期比＋2.4％（４-６月期：▲0.8％）と持ち
直している。グローバルなＡＩ関連需要の拡大を背景に、半導体製造装置などの資本財輸出が好
調だったほか、４-６月期に大きく落ち込んだ中東向け輸出に回復の動きがみられたことも財輸出

の押し上げ要因になった。サービス輸出の特殊要因による振れを除けば、海外景気の底堅さとＡ
Ｉ関連需要の拡大を背景に、輸出は緩やかな増加基調を維持していると評価できる。
先行きについても、米国を中心とした海外景気の底堅さに加え、グローバルなＡＩ関連需要の
拡大が半導体製造装置や電子部品などの輸出を下支えすることから、緩やかな増加基調が続くと
予想する。
輸入は前期比＋2.5％と増加した。４-６月期には資源輸入の激減により輸入が減少していた
が、代替調達の進展により７-９月期は明確に持ち直した。この結果、外需寄与度は前期比
▲0.6％Pt（前期比年率寄与度▲2.4％Pt）と大幅なマイナスになった。
ただし、輸入増加による成長率の押し下げは、原油などの供給環境が改善したことに伴うもの
であり、需要の弱さを示すものではない。また、輸入増加の裏側では、国家備蓄原油の取り崩し
が一服したことに伴って公的在庫変動が前期比で＋0.5％ポイント、前期比年率では＋2.0％ポイ
ントの大幅プラス寄与となっている。両者は一体として評価する必要があるだろう。

民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.0％Ptと、成長率にはほぼニュートラルだった。製品在庫と原
材料在庫が押し上げに寄与した一方、流通在庫と仕掛品在庫が下押し要因になった。
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％と、２四半期連続で減少した。建設コストの上昇が下押し要因となっ
ており、住宅投資を取り巻く環境は引き続き厳しい。先行きについても、建設コストの高止まり
や金利上昇の影響から、低調な推移が続く可能性が高い。
政府消費は前期比▲0.0％と概ね横ばいとなった。４-６月期に前期比＋1.5％と高い伸びとなっ
た後、増勢が一服している。もっとも、医療・介護関連の支出などは趨勢的に増加しており、政
府消費の弱さは前期の反動による一時的なものと考えられる。
公共投資は前期比▲1.1％と減少した。政府の国土強靱化関連工事などが下支えにはなっている
が、建設コストの上昇が実質公共投資の下押し要因になっているとみられる。

### Page 5

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

eMemory Technology Inc  | Asia Pacific
Security demand resilient amid
cloud slowdown
Record earnings: 3Q revenue was NT$1.21bn (+10.4% Q/Q, +27.1% Y/Y) and
attributable net income was NT$639mn (+10.2% Q/Q, +31.2% Y/Y), 2.7% and 1.6%
above MSe, respectively. Operating income beat MSe by 7.0%, while margin reached
63.2%, 2.6ppt above MSe. Royalties rose 15.4% Q/Q, with 12-inch royalties up 20.2%,
versus 3.5% opex growth. PUF security revenue grew 213% Y/Y to 14.5% of sales.

EPS was NT$8.56.
Royalty outlook: Management expects growth to accelerate on higher advanced-
node rates, product ramps and PUF adoption. Customers favor fewer, higher-value
flagship designs, with advanced-node licensing and royalty values several times
those of mature nodes. Management provided no quantified 4Q guidance.
Security expansion: PUFrt adoption in Arm Neoverse CSS V3, BMC, SSD and
networking broadens data-center exposure. Penetration, content per chip and node

migration support growth, while PQC adds license value. Some 3nm designs are in
production, with 2nm qualification and initial adoption targeted for 2027. PUF-
integrated 1T Flash remains in foundry development, with no launch date. PUF
accounts for only 1.8% of royalties, leaving upside dependent on conversion to
production.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
eMemory Technology Inc (3529.TWO)
We derive our price target from a residual income model. We assume an 8.6% cost of equity
(2.0% risk-free rate, 6.0% equity risk premium, and 1.1 beta), a payout ratio of 90%, a medium-
term earnings growth rate of 23.0%, and a terminal growth rate of 4.4%. Based on these
assumptions, our RI model implies an equity value of NT$249bn, or NT$3,330 per share.
Risks to Upside

n Faster-than-expected PUF adoption
n Stronger-than-expected advanced-node OTP migration
n Higher-than-expected royalty expansion from wafer value growth
Risks to Downside
n Slower-than-expected adoption by key customers
n Slower-than-expected PQC adoption
n Increasing geopolitical and trade policy risks

### Page 1

Merida (9914.TW)
3Q Briefing Takeaway – New Model Cycle to Drive 2027 Recovery; SBC 
Visibility Remains Limited 
CITI'S TAKE 
We expect Merida’s core business to recover in 2027E, supported by 
accelerating new model launches and largely normalized EU inventories. 
However, we expect the recovery in 2027E to be gradual with US demand 
remaining sluggish. Meanwhile, despite improving pre-tax profits, SBC’s 
profitability remains highly uncertain. We trim our TP to NT$67 factoring in 
a lowered NTM P/E of 11x and earnings cuts. Remain Neutral as we see 
limited near-term catalysts and prefer to await clearer evidence of 
sustainable SBC profitability and stronger core earnings momentum.
Outlook: More new model launches in 2027E to drive a sales comeback — Mgmt. 
attributed softer shipments this year to deliberate reductions in existing models 
ahead of more new product launches in 2027E. Both Merida’s own brand and its 
major OEM customer, SBC, have adopted this strategy, aiming to preserve pricing 
discipline and avoid excessive discounting. Mgmt. expects the inventory 
adjustment to conclude by year-end and most new models are scheduled to begin 
shipping from Jan-27, paving the way for a sales recovery in 2027E.  
EU leads the recovery; China stabilizes while the US remains sluggish — Merida’s 
own brand grew 14% y/y in 9M26 in EU, supported by solid demand for road and 
gravel bikes. The mgmt. believe inventory normalization is largely complete in EU. 
China’s own brand sales grew 1% y/y in 9M26, better than the original expectation 
of DD% decline, helped primarily by entry and mid end bikes. Mgmt. expects another 
single digit sales growth in China in 2027E, supported by resilient commuter 
demand. The recovery of US market, on the other hand, is lagging EU and China amid 
inflationary pressure and persisting macro uncertainties.   
Still low visibility on SBC profitability —The weak bottom-line of SBC was primarily 
due to a conservative effective tax rate assumptions rather than a deterioration of 
its fundamentals. SBC’s 9M26 pre-tax profits grew 42% y/y or +20% y/y excluding 
US tariff refunds, reflecting improving operating performance and cost discipline. 
While improving pre-tax profitability is encouraging, we remain cautious on SBC’s 
near-term earnings contribution until there is a greater clarity on its normalized 
effective tax rate and the sustainability of US profitability. (continued)

Earnings revisions and implications — We lower our earnings estimates by 20%
for 2026E and 6% for 2027E/2028E, mainly reflecting weaker-than-expected core
sales and profitability, alongside continued uncertainty surrounding SBC’s
profitability. We cut our TP to NT$67 based on 11x 4Q26-3Q27E P/E, down from
15.5x, factoring in weaker-than-expected core business and low visibility of SBC.
While we acknowledge the potential for a stronger sales recovery in 2027E as

Merida accelerates its new model launches, we believe the recovery will be gradual
rather than a meaningful acceleration in the underlying market demand.
Meanwhile, SBC’s uncertain net profitability and lingering US market weakness
remain the key overhang.  We would prefer to stay on the sidelines, awaiting clearer
evidence of sustainable SBC net profitability and stronger earnings momentum
from the core business. Reiterate Neutral.

Bull/Bear: Merida (9914.TW)

### Page 5

Merida (9914.TW)
17 November 2026 Citi Research
5
Merida
Company description
Merida is the second-largest bike manufacturer in Taiwan. Founded in 1972,
Merida was initially involved in OEM manufacturing before it started
marketing bikes under its own Merida brand in 1986. Merida has expanded its
footprint globally through its Merida brand and three OEM brands:
Specialized (in which Merida has a 35% stake), Centurion (51% stake), and
Miyata (45%). Merida's own brand contributes around 30-40% of total

revenues. Merida has established a proprietary network of stores throughout
China, with 16 wholly owned subsidiaries and close to 2,000 retailers that sell
only Merida-branded bikes.
Investment strategy
We rate Merida shares as Neutral. Although bike inventories have normalised
in the major markets after aggressive clearance over the past 2 years, slower-
than-expected bike demand recovery continues to weigh on Merida's topline
momentum. The EU market appears to be where we should see better growth

for 2026E, while China market also turns better than expected from 2Q26; yet
with low visibility of SBC profit trend and limited positive catalysts in sight,
we expect the share price to be range bound.
Valuation
Our target price for Merida shares of NT$93 is based on a 4Q26-3Q27E P/E of
11x, a 50% discount to its historical average of 22x during pre-Covid years
(2014-2019), in view of soft order momentum as a result of slow recovery in
bike demand, a volatile margin trend, and low visibility of SBC.

Risks
Downside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade below our target
price include: 1) e-bike adoption slower than expected; 2) weaker restocking
demand; 3) lukewarm sell-through at SBC; and 4) a weaker macro.
Upside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade above our target price
include: 1) e-bike adoption faster than expected; 2) stronger restocking
demand; 3) stronger sell-through at SBC; and 4) a strong macro.
If you are visually impaired and would like to speak to a Citi representative regarding the details

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com

貿聯 3Q26 財 報 優 於 預 期 ， 營 業 利 益 率 創 歷 史 新 高 ， 加 上 
Interplex 併表效益優於原先預估，為後續營運增添新亮點。展
望未來，研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，主要考量：(1) 隨 
AI 機櫃功耗持續提升，電力傳輸規格朝高電流、高壓化發展，帶
動 Power Whip、Busbar 等產品升級，單櫃電源產值可望倍增；
(2) 高速互連朝 1.6T 以上演進，AEC 與高階光纖業務接續放
量，推升資料傳輸產值；(3) Interplex 併購補足精密機構能力，
結合既有電力及高速互連布局，貿聯可望由零組件供應商逐步延
伸至機櫃次系統整合，擴大單一客戶供應範圍及長期成長商機。
研究部將 Interplex 納入預估，上修 2026、2027 年稀釋後 EPS 
至 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）及 145.24 元
（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建
置不確定性升高，可能使新平台導入與機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑
評價表現，研究部將本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍，以 2027 年 
EPS 為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至
「強力買進」。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營業利益率創歷史新高，財報優於預期 
貿聯 3Q26 營收 278.75 億元（+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY），符
合預期。其中 HPC 營收成長強勁（+30% QoQ、+61% YoY），營收
占比由 2Q26 的 42% 提升至 46%。隨 AEC 拉貨恢復正常，上半
年產線未達經濟規模的不利影響逐步消除，帶動整體毛利率回升
至 32.53%（+1.80ppt QoQ、−0.72ppt YoY）；加上營收規模擴
大，營業費用率降至 11.75%（−1.76ppt QoQ、−1.97ppt YoY），
推升營業利益率至 20.78%（+3.56ppt QoQ、+1.24ppt YoY），創
歷史新高。營業利益達 57.92 億元（+44.5% QoQ、+61.0% YoY），
優於原估的 52.50 億元。業外因匯兌損失及利息費用合計損失 
2.30 億元，稅後淨利達 42.90 億元（+43.9% QoQ、+63.2% YoY），
優於原估的 39.78 億元，稀釋後 EPS 為 20.34 元，優於原估
的 19.45 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. Interplex 併表貢獻超預期，上修 4Q26 營收與獲利 
貿聯 10 月營收 118.46 億元（+26.9% MoM、+80.0% YoY），創
歷史新高，主要受惠 Interplex 於 9 月底完成交割，10 月首
度完整併表即貢獻營收 6,700 萬美元，約為併購公告時近 12 個
月平均月營收的一倍。Interplex 為 NVIDIA NVLink Switch 機
殼主要供應商，受惠 AI 機櫃需求強勁，併表貢獻高於原先預估。
考量併購效益優於預期，加上既有業務持續成長，研究部上修 
4Q26 營收至 349.76 億元（+25.5% QoQ、+76.8% YoY，原估 
302.69 億元）。預估毛利率為 32.49%（−0.04ppt QoQ、+0.56ppt 
YoY），大致持平前季；隨營收規模擴大及 Interplex 較低費用
率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.85%，推升營業利益率至 21.64%
（+0.86ppt QoQ、+3.27ppt YoY，原估 20.0%）。預估 4Q26 稅
後淨利達 55.28 億元（+28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY），稀釋後 EPS 
由原估 22.38 元上修至 26.21 元（ +28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY）。

3. AI 機櫃供電架構升級，電源單櫃產值與市占率同步提升 
隨 AI 伺服器運算密度持續提高，機櫃功耗由 GB200 世代約 
120kW，朝 Vera Rubin 世代 200kW 以上發展，電力傳輸規格同
步升級。在既有低壓直流架構下，功耗提升帶動電流需求增加，
Power Whip 與 Busbar 須升級至更高載流規格，推升單櫃電源
產值；隨機櫃功率持續攀升，傳統 54VDC 架構面臨銅材用量、配
電損耗及機櫃空間限制，NVIDIA 已推動 800VDC 高壓直流架構，
透過提高配電電壓、減少電力轉換環節，支援未來 MW 級 AI 機
櫃需求。貿聯除受惠電源產品規格升級，於 Vera Rubin 世代的 
Busbar 市占率亦可望明顯提升，推動單櫃電源產值較 GB 世代
倍增。考量新平台相關出貨預計於 1H27 起逐季放量，研究部預
估 2027 年電源營收成長約 114% YoY；中長期隨 800VDC 架構
逐步導入，既有 Power Whip、Busbar 及高電流連接技術可望延
伸至新一代配電系統，持續擴大供應商機。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. 高速互連升級至 1.6T，AEC 與高階光纖雙軌成長 
隨 AI 資料中心傳輸頻寬由 800G 朝 1.6T 以上升級，高速互連
對訊號完整性、傳輸距離及功耗的要求持續提高。短距離傳輸仍以
銅纜具成本優勢，較長距離則由光纖滿足頻寬與訊號傳輸需求，預
期 AEC 與光互連將持續並存。AEC 方面，1.6T 新規格因材料成本
及組裝難度提高，產品單價與毛利率可望優於前代，預計於 4Q26 
至 1Q27 逐步導入量產，研究部預估 2027 年 AEC 營收成長約 
41% YoY，主要受惠高階產品占比提升。光纖方面，新富生主要供
應 AI 資料中心機櫃間傳輸所需的 MPO、MT 高密度光纖跳線及連
接器組件，受惠高速光纖互連需求成長，月營收較 2026 年初成長
逾一倍，獲利同步改善。中長期來看，貿聯亦積極布局 Optical 
Shuffle、CPO 交換器方案及 Micro-VCSEL 光互連技術，逐步由傳
統線纜組裝延伸至高階光學組件，為後續成長開拓更多商機。

圖一、貿聯-KY 1Q25～4Q27F HPC 營收結構

資料來源：統一投顧預估

5. 半導體設備需求強勁，非 HPC 業務維持穩健 
貿聯 3Q26 半導體設備營收成長 11% QoQ、70% YoY，為非 HPC 業
務主要成長動能。隨先進製程及半導體設備投資持續推進，客戶需
求由傳統線束延伸至設備模組、精密組裝及次系統整合，帶動單機
供應產值提升。貿聯已深入薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備供應鏈，並逐步拓
展至微影及檢測設備，擴大產品應用範圍。其他業務方面，工廠自
動化、醫療、能源及車用等終端需求維持穩健，提供多元營收來源。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

6. 上修 2026～2028 年獲利預估，營運槓桿與併購效益持續擴
大 
研究部將 Interplex 併表貢獻納入預估，上修貿聯 2026 年營收
至 1,069.96 億元（+50.2% YoY，原估 1,021.18 億元），毛利率
估 31.4%，稀釋後 EPS 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）。
展望 2027 年，受惠 AI 電源及高速互連業務持續成長，加上 
Interplex 全年併表，預估營收達 1,710.84 億元（+59.9% YoY，
原估 1,517.46 億元）。隨高毛利 HPC 業務占比提升，毛利率可
望升至 34.3%（原估 34.0%）；加上營收規模擴大及 Interplex 
較低費用率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.2%（原估 13.0%），推升營
業利益率至 24.1%（原估 21.0%），稀釋後 EPS 上修至 145.24 
元（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。展望 2028 年，隨 AI 機
櫃供電架構朝 800VDC 演進、高速互連規格持續升級，加上 
Interplex 受惠機櫃需求成長及精密機構件供應範圍擴大，預期將
共同帶動營運成長。研究部預估營收達 2,136.69 億元（+24.9% 
YoY），稀釋後 EPS 196.20 元（+35.1% YoY，原估 171.03 元）。

圖二、貿聯-KY 2025～2028F 各應用別營收占比

資料來源：公司資料及統一投顧預估

7. 中長期成長趨勢明確，投資評等調升至「強力買進」 
研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，隨 AI 機櫃電力與高速互
連規格持續升級，帶動單櫃供應產值提升，加上 Interplex 補足
精密機構能力，逐步擴大機櫃次系統供應範圍，有利深化客戶合作
關係並提升市占率。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建置不確定性升高，
可能使新平台導入及機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑市場評價，研究部將
本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍。以 2027 年稀釋後 EPS 145.24 
元為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至「強
力買進」。

附錄一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等 定義 
強力買進    預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上 
買進 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10%以上 
中立 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10% 
降低持股 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於(-10%)以下 
未評等 沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

附 錄 二 ： 免 責 宣 言

司 認 可 之 資 料來源 ， 並 基 於 特 定 日 期 所 做 之 判 斷 ， 但 不 保 證 其 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 ， 報
告 中 所 有 的 意 見 及 預 估 ， 如 有 變 更 恕 不 另 行 通 知 。

供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與

### Page 1

2026.10.29   
HD현대중공업  (329180)

3Q26: 조선사는 우직해야 한다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 5조 7,536억원/영업이익 8,992억원 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 매출은 5조 7,536억원/영업이익은 8,992억원으로 
컨센서스(6조 1,295억원/9,671억원) 및 당사 추정치(6조 859억원/9,679억원) 대비 
매출 및 영업이익 각각 -6.1%/-7.0%, -5.5%/-7.1%를 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다.  
조선사업부 매출이 4조 3,988억원/영업이익 6,463억원(OPM 14.7%)이며 이중 상선 
OPM은 14.7%로, 2분기 17.8%에 비해 3.1%P 하락하였으나 1분기 15.9%와 크게 
차이나지 않는 수준을 유지하면서 조업일수 및 환율 하락에도 불구하고 P-MIX및 
빈티지 개선에 따른 효과를 보여주었다. 해양사업부는 매출 3,799억원/영업이익 
1,265억원(OPM 33.3%)로 지난 분기(15.9%)보다도 훨씬 높은 영업이익을 
기록하였다. 엔진기계사업부는 매출 9,511억원/영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)를 
기록하며 역시 견조한 실적을 기록하였다.  
일회성 이익으로 Trion 공사의 C/O 180억원, 일회성 손실로는 셧다운에 따른 
조업감소 영향 500억원 및 환율하락에 따른 이익 감소분 450억원이 있었으며 금액은 
공개되지 않았으나 임단협 잠정타결에 따른 성과급/타결금 지급에 대한 추가 충당금의 
75% 인식이 반영되었다. 알려진 일회성만 제거하더라도 실질 영업이익률은 
17%이상으로 2분기 16.4%보다 높아진 호실적이라고 할 수 있다. 특히 RUYA 공사가 
본 궤도에 오르면서 해양사업부의 이익률이 괄목할 만큼 높아졌으며 지속가능하다는 
회사의 설명이다.

투자의견 BUY 및 조선업종 TOP PICK 유지, 목표주가 530,000원 하향 
HD현대중공업의 목표주가를 530,000원으로 38.3% 하향하지만, 투자의견 BUY와 
조선업종 TOP PICK을 유지한다. 환율 하락 및 28년 하반기 엔진증설 매출확대, 
해양공사 이익률 상승 등 요인을 종합 반영하여 실적 추정치를 미세 반영하여 도출한 
2027F BPS 131,910원에 Target P/B를 기존대비 하향한 4.0배로 적용하였다. 특수선 
기대감 및 선가상승 기대감 하락에 따른 조선업종 전반의 밸류에이션 하락을 반영하여 
Target을 낮췄지만 그럼에도 조선업종 중 가장 높은 멀티플을 부여하며 Top 
Pick의견을 견지한다. 여러 악재에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업은 탄탄하다. 본업인 
상선사업부의 9월말 기준 수주량은 137.9억 달러로 목표치를 이미 초과 달성하였으며 
전사 기준으로도 수주목표 90.5%를 채워넣었다. 수주잔고 또한 상선 421억 
달러/전사 609억 달러로 각각 작년 말 대비 12.6%/13.2% 증가하였다. 해양부문과 
엔진기계부문은 조선사업부보다도 높은 이익률로 든든한 실적의 기반이 되고 있다.  
최근 HD현대그룹은 미국의 EPC강자 Kiewit과 미국 합작법인 설립에 관한 계약을 
맺고 그린필드 기반의 미국 신규 조선소 건설을 추진하고 있다. 빠르면 올해 중으로 
건립과 관련한 세부 사항이 공개될 예정이며 HD현대중공업이 중추적인 역할을 담당할 
것으로 보인다. 일찍부터 브라운필드 방식으로 접근한 동종사와 달리 다소 시간은 
걸렸지만 과감하게 결정된 투자다. 조선소 예상 준공 역시 2028년 이후로 예상되어 
당장 기업가치에 반영하기는 어려우나 엔진 증설과 함께 2028년 후에는 분명한 실적 
상향 요인이 될 것으로 예상한다. 우직하게 본업에 충실하며 신중하게 미래를 준비하는 
조선소, 귀감이다.

 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
SK 하이닉스 최대주주 
SK 하이닉스 4 분기도 배당 주당 20,000 원 이상  
SK 하이닉스는 전일 주당 20,000 원(실적반영: 10,000 원, 키옥시아 특별배당: 
10,000원)의 3분기 배당을 발표하였다. 키옥시아 특별배당은 3분기 배당과 26년 회
계연도 결산배당으로 나누어 지급예정이다. 이에 SK 스퀘어는 2조 9,220억원(1분기
와 2분기 배당수입은 각 548억원)의 배당수입을 수취하게 된다. SK하이닉스는 26년 
전체 현금 배당 규모는 30조원 이상을 발표했는데, 3분기까지 지급하는 배당금은 14.6
조원이기 때문에 4분기에도 최소한 주당 20.000원 이상의 배당이 예상된다. 이 경우 
SK 스퀘어의 26 년 배당수입은 6 조원에 달할 것으로 예상한다. SK 스퀘어는 ‘26 년
~’28년 경상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 주주환원정책을 보유하고 있
어 SK스퀘어의 현 시가총액 대비 27년 주주환원율은 최소 1.5% 이상 될 전망이다.

SK 하이닉스 자사주 취득·소각으로 높아질 지배력 
SK하이닉스는 20조원의 자사주 매입을 발표하였다. 이 중 10조원은 임직원 보상목적
이며, 10조원은 주주환원 목적이다. 26년 총 60조원 이상의 자사주 취득·소각 예정(8
월 결의분 40 조원 포함)으로 임직원 보상 목적의 자사주 매입은 제외되기 때문에 26
년 실적 확정 후 추가적으로 10조원 이상의 자사주 취득이 예상된다. 기 취득한 자사주 
40조원(3.3%)는 11월 5일 소각 예정으로 소각 이후 SK스퀘어의 SK하이닉스 지분
율은 20.5%(2 분기말 기준)에서 21.2%로 상승하게 된다. 이번 발표한 10 조원
(1.0%) 자사주 소각시 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 21.4%이 되며, 추가로 10 조원을 추가 
소각한다면 21.7%까지 지분율이 상승하게 된다.

SK 하이닉스 대비 높은 베타 
SK스퀘어에 대한 투자의견 매수를 유지한다. SK스퀘어 NAV의 98%가량을 차지하
는 SK하이닉스의 현금배당 증가로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 재원이 확대되고, SK하이닉
스의 자사주 취득·소각으로 지배력이 강화되기 때문이다. SK하이닉스는 전일 컨퍼런스 
콜을 통해 29년까지 D램과 HBM 물량이 사실상 모두 솔드아웃된 상황이라 밝히며 주
가 반등 가능성이 높은데, SK 스퀘어 베타(1.8)는 SK 하이닉스(1.7) 대비 높기 때문에 
SK 하이닉스 주가 반등에 따른 SK 스퀘어의 동반 주가 상승을 기대할 수 있다. 현재 
SK스퀘어의 NAV 대비 할인율은 46.5%이며, SK하이닉스가 자사주 60조원 소각시 
할인율은 49.4%까지 상승하게 된다.

2026-10-29

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

2 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Oil Price Dynamics Remain Key to the Outlook
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Inflation, interest rates and equity market dynamics have changed materially since mid-
August, as widening attacks on Middle East and Russian crude oil and refining capacity
worsened supply and price conditions. Indeed, with the oil market already strained, an
early-September attack on a major Saudi Arabian export pipeline sharply raised concerns

about a protracted and deepening oil crisis. While Middle East crude exports eventually
managed to significantly rebound, increased security risks and freight costs raised
physical crude prices.
Against this backdrop, the spot Brent price surged from $92/barrel in mid-August
back into a $115 to $135/barrel price range until recently. Reflecting unusual market
tightness, other North Sea oil grades were recently trading even higher, up to $160/
barrel, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). Even as it cooled from $135

to $115/barrel between October 2 and October 20, spot Brent is still almost double
year-ago levels, and so are many diesel prices. The AAA average U.S. retail diesel price
recently hit a new record and was 74% higher year-over-year (YoY) as of October 20.
With insufficient regional supply, the New York Harbor wholesale diesel price is 130%
higher from a year ago, according to the Energy Information Administration (EIA).
Indeed, sharply higher oil prices and a growing diesel shortage have put the global diesel

market under extreme strain. The U.S., for example, has little distillate cushion and
limited spare refining capacity. The distillate inventory (diesel and fuel oil) has decreased
from 37 days of demand coverage in January to just 26 days by early October, according
to the EIA. This 30% drop brought the supply buffer to the bottom of its historical
range. With limited refining capacity and reliance on imports for about 10% of regional
consumption, East Coast distillate inventories are the lowest since 1990, for example,

and are expected to remain below their 5-year average into late 2027, according to EIA
data. More broadly, according to the IEA’s October Oil Market Report (OMR), “oil market
balances are forecast to remain in deficit through the end of the year, with constrained
middle distillate supplies likely to persist well into 2027.”
Thus, although wage growth/labor demand do not appear inflationary, the oil crisis
risks continuing to feed “core” inflation. According to the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book1

covering precisely the mid-August to early-October period of heightened oil-market
turmoil, “higher oil prices permeated all District reports,” with firms noting that fuel
surcharges were passing on increased costs throughout the supply chain. The 22% rise
in airfares over the past year alone contributed around 0.2 percentage point (pp) to the
2.6% 12-month increase in the “core” consumer price index (CPI) for September. Aside
from transportation costs effects, the spike in diesel prices also portends high food

prices with a few-month lag given planting/harvesting cost considerations.
By solidifying expectations for higher Fed interest rates to fight inflation, the surge in oil
prices has contributed to the spike in long-term Treasury yields this year. According to an
October 13, 2026, Empirical Research Partners report, the Iran-war oil crisis is estimated
to have contributed about 0.50 pp to the roughly 2.40 pp increase in the real U.S. long-
term Treasury rate from 2020 to early October (Exhibit 1A). According to the report, this

oil-price effect accounts for “much of the rise in real rates since February 27, 2026...”
On the positive side, while most other underpinnings for higher rates compared to the
pre-pandemic decade are structural (Exhibit 1A), the oil-price-related effect on interest
rates is reversible, implying that a resolution to the current oil crisis has the potential to
help lower both inflation and interest rates, relieving some of the current headwinds to
growth and asset prices.

Indeed, while much of the increase in interest rates since the pandemic reflects stronger,
more normal economic growth, and has thus been accompanied by surging corporate
1 Summary of Commentary on Current Economic Conditions by Federal Reserve District, Federal Reserve, October 2026.
Investment Implications
Oil market conditions remain a swing
factor for the outlook. A shift lower in
oil risk premia and improvement on
the refining front would go a long way

toward easing inflation and interest
rates while bolstering risk-asset
appetite. A gradual increase in Fixed
Income portfolio duration continues to
make sense, in our view.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
earnings and risk-asset prices, worsening oil-market conditions since mid-summer and
associated spike in yields have been accompanied by a loss of momentum in U.S. equity
prices (Exhibit 1B). Given their higher sensitivity to energy price and interest rates shocks,
Small-caps lost relatively more momentum than Large-caps during this period.
The Large-cap rally also faded, though, as oil and interest rate headwinds widened stock-

level return dispersion, with roughly 40% of constituent stocks in bear market territory
(down 20% or more). Still, buoyed by the Technology sector as well as by expectations
of conflict resolution, the market-capitalization weighted S&P 500 Index has mainly
consolidated just below its peak. While the equal-weighted S&P 500 Index stumbled more,
it was just about 3% off its peak as of October 20.
While the economy remains resilient and profit expectations remain strong, persistent

oil market turmoil makes the macro backdrop more fragile. For example, the Beige Book
not only highlights concern about spreading fuel-related inflation, as noted above, but its
language also suggests that economic activity lost some momentum since its mid-July
to late-August report. Overall, it describes growth as slight to moderate, with generally
moderate manufacturing growth except for robust activity in the energy-heavy Dallas
District. Little growth is reported in other sectors overall, with most Districts seeing little

to no change in consumer spending, for example.
Employment was described as stable to little changed, with generally adequate labor supply
except for certain specialized trade workers. Five of twelve Districts note some reports of
businesses cutting staff through attrition, delaying hiring or anticipating less job creation
as a result of AI-driven productivity gains. Most Districts reported that business maintained
a positive outlook for coming months, though “expectations tend to be for slight growth,

weighted toward manufacturing and burdened by persistent uncertainty.”
The September employment and retail sales reports seem to validate the anecdotal Beige
Book evidence. Also, the 0.4% annualized manufacturing production growth over the July to
September period (and 1.1% YoY, according to the Federal Reserve Broad), while welcome,
is hardly an indication of booming conditions. Against a backdrop of rising electricity prices,
wage growth lagging inflation, and higher gasoline prices broadly offsetting this year’s tax

cut benefits, real consumer spending is unlikely to accelerate much absent stronger labor
demand and real wage growth. A resolution to the oil crisis that brings economic relief and
tempers interest rates is thus critical for continued expansion in 2027, in our view.

Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings have generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than two-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.”2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market.2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of investment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractive in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market moves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting government
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff
Source: Chief Investment Office. Data as of October 22, 2026.
* “Pop the Champagne,” Portfolio Insights, Chief Investment Office, August 2026.

6 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

► 投資人持續擔憂AI科技發展，儘管輝達財報
優於預期，然代理執行長強調資本配置紀律
後，近期市場焦點圍繞在未來數年AI資本支
出下修與獲利成長放緩風險，疊加Anthropic
上市募集鉅資後，股價亦牽動AI估值變化。
市場恐慌情緒持續擴大，加重科技相關類股
與美國費城半導體指數持續修正，跌勢亦擴

散至非科技類股，僅部分具防禦性質如必需
消費與公用事業類股跌幅較小。
► 川普與會APEC亞太經合會，並與中國國家
主席習近平及俄羅斯總統普丁會面，中國宣
布將出資2,000萬美元予APEC並強調亞太共
同體，然早前美國商務部工業暨安全局公告
先進半導體遠端存取安全立法，管制中國企
業透過中國以外第三地海外雲端服務取得先
進運算資源，美中兩大國對AI科技競爭態勢
白熱化，先進晶片出口管制趨嚴亦加重科技
潛在擾動，疊加聯準會緊縮貨幣政策路徑與
地緣衝突存不確定性，市場波動恐延續。

遇襲事件，然美國能源資訊管理局(EIA)公布
上週美國商業原油庫存大增340萬桶並高於
市場預估，原油供過於求擔憂與風險偏好降
溫造成油價走弱，另聯準會升息預期與美元
走升，亦對金價帶來一定程度壓力。

輝達
股價(左軸) 預估本益比(右軸)

資料來源：Bloomberg、凱基證券整理
► 輝達2027財年第三季財報(營收、EPS、數據
中心)超預期，營收較去年同期翻倍以上、季
增20%。資料中心營收單季季增21%至1,076
億美元，大規模雲端服務商貢獻600億。
► 董事會表示黃仁勳已辭職不會復任，交接存
在不確定性，對公司潛在影響：1)輝達動用
自身強勁獲利能力，支持AI業界，市場質疑
輝達收入持續性，接替者出現前，融資策略
可能偏保守；2) 事 件 引 發 加 強 出 口 管 制 憂
慮，新管理層或缺乏在政府遊說的能力，收
緊出口可能衝擊向亞洲出貨，拖累獲利。
► 公司EPS年增翻倍，毛利率維持75%，但預
估本益比從2024年40倍持續回落至現時不足
11倍，輝達若長期高速增長，市場應給予更
高估值。管理層變動令評價更低，但事件曝
光前，預估本益比已跌至約15倍。輝達AI訓

練與推理有領導地位，產能鎖定具優勢，但
市占率正流失予客製化晶片，疊加民主黨偏
向放慢AI發展，可能令未來晶片需求放慢。
輝達財報具韌性，交接存不確定性，市場早已不願提高評價

### Page 6

6
► Claude AI模型開發商Anthropic上市，IPO認
股價達83美元高於先前目標區間，加上私募
260億美元，總募資資金逾1,000億美元超過
SpaceX上市紀錄，凱基投顧預估Anthropic
成長動能來自：(1)訂閱與用量制帶動營收至
2030年複合成長近4成；(2)儘管市場擔憂降
價問題，然算力需求仍緊且Anthropic第一層
級付費Token市佔明顯高於其他AI模型開發
商，(3)算力成本認列早於營收，預估2027年
首季為獲利相對低點，2026年經濟FCF就轉
正且後續將逐年增長。
► Anthropic企業用戶淨收入留存率(NRR)高達
9倍，展現對企業客戶強大吸引力，未來最大
營收來源為企業將更多工作交付AI執行，而
非更多人訂閱AI，然仍須留意近期科技擾動
形塑AI短期逆風，後續風險觀察企業AI預算
是 否 下 滑( 市場終端需求) 、 第一層級付費
Token市佔比重是否快速下滑(AI模型市場地
位鬆動)及實收均價年減幅是否連2年高於推
論成本(降價開始由利潤吸收)等負面因素。

### Page 7

投資焦點
► 聯準會會議記錄顯示所有官員認為景氣若按
預期態勢發展，後續仍有升息必要，部分官
員擔憂通膨較先前更加頑固，應採取較原先
更緊縮的升息步伐，有兩位官員亦表示若通
膨朝不利方向發展，可能需升息2碼，利率市
場持續定價反映更鷹派貨幣政策路徑。
► 財政赤字方面，民主黨掌握國會兩院將進入
分立政府，可能阻止共和黨推出更多支出法
案，強制性財政支出佔總支出6成且佔比將續
升，而強制性支出僅能以立法縮減，民主黨
國會席次未達三分之二，任何立法將受總統
否決權鉗制，美國財政紀律難見明顯好轉。
► 考量聯準會升息預期持續、美國政府債務累
積和利息支出成為財政最大隱憂，以及明年
新債務上限協商將形塑美國公債市場焦點，
另AI發債潮和川普政策不確定性持續，研判
上述美國公債多項結構利空短期難消散，待
聯準會最快明年上半年停止升息且利率見高
後，再對美國公債進行配置。
多數聯準會官員言論維持鷹派論調市場對聯準會明年升息3碼預期仍在
聯準會認證景氣穩，高利率環境恐持續至明年上半年

投資焦點投資級債
► 聯準會確認景氣態勢仍強，近月美國科技大
廠蘋果與微軟聯袂發債融資，受大額債券供
給與市場風險偏好降溫影響，推升美國投資
級債殖利率持續揚升，預期明年AI資本支出
持續，整體美國投資級企業發債量將維持歷
史相對高位，債市投資仍以中短天期投資級
債為布局首選，待明年上半年利率升至高位
後，再逐步增加中長天期債部位配置。
► 美國大型企業獲利體現高成長動能，今年來
投資級 債企 業財 務 槓桿持 穩且信 用利 差微
升，體現在每單位槓桿利差僅略為上行，考

量債信基本面仍佳有望限制利差擴大幅度；
建議佈局A級至BBB級公司債、並聚焦風險
調整後正利差者如銀行、非必需消費、公用
事業和通訊產業鎖住好息，亦可留意能源、
必需消費、公用事業和資本設備與科技債相
關性較低產業分散風險，科技投資級債雖債
信體質穩健且違約風險有限，然逢大額發債
潮不建議交易，僅以持有至到期為主。
財務槓桿持穩而利差微升，反映在債信指標上AI企業持續融資加劇信用市場擾動
企業發債將持續，留意利率上行時鎖定好債

9
股市
 黃仁勳相關事件恐逐步強化美國對AI晶片出口管制、AI資料中心建設進程或將受到州政府緊縮政策
而造成評價面壓力並增添股價波動。短期可留意防禦股以增強抗震力以抵禦市場震盪。長期而言，
AI從全面看好轉向選股，且大優於小，市場波瀾過後產業長期正向趨勢未變。當前則可布局財務健
全度較高的高品質價值股，以獲取穩定報酬並降低市場波動影響。
 歐、日股可分散配置，利率走升有利銀行股。此外，隨財政政策支持，歐洲國防軍工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，AI從
全面看好轉向選股、且大優於小，市場波
瀾後長期正向趨勢未變，當前可布局財務
健全度較高的高品質價值股
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲國防
債市
 美伊談判走向反覆未明，油價維持高檔震盪。CPI物價高於預期，強化市場對聯準會升息步伐預估，
聯準會官員立場維持較高利率抑制通膨發展。建議以中短天期債券投資為主，可逢利率走升時布局
進行鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，
包含金融、非必需消費、通訊及公用事業等產業。

債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、非必需消費、通訊及公用事業等
匯市
 CPI通膨支撐升息預期，美元走勢偏強。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期偏鷹，日圓弱勢盤整。
美元高檔整理
商品
 美伊談判走向反覆，油價一度回落但再度反彈，短線仍偏震盪。
 去美元化支撐全球央行買盤，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
低接買盤流入，黃金中長期維持偏多看法

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。

基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書(或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用(境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金(不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施)；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

Document 432: Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 16
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Tracking Europe’s AI transition:  
A new STOXX 600 earnings-call 
framework

1 
Authors 
Michael Kirker 
Economist

November 11, 2026 
How quickly is AI moving from experimentation into everyday business, and 
where are companies reporting tangible gains? The answers matter for 
institutional investors and policymakers assessing productivity, operating 
efficiency and labour demand. Yet comparable evidence across Europe remains 
limited, making it difficult to track the pace and scale of the transition. 1 
We address this gap with a proprietary framework that turns what is said on 
STOXX 600 earnings call into three structured indicators: AI adoption 
intensity, reported productivity benefits, and the impact on workforces. 
Large language models analyse what management teams say about AI’s 
operational role, applying a common scoring framework across companies, 
sectors and countries. Our new approach provides a timely pan-European 
comparable tracker that captures not just whether AI is being mentioned, but its 
real-world impact as the adoption of the new technology unfolds. 
Key Takeaways: 
— Adoption is broadening, with signs of faster progress. AI-use scores rose 
gradually from a low base before strengthening over the past 12 months. The 
increase extends across sectors, although the pace and level of adoption 
remain uneven. 
— Workforce impacts remain limited in corporate disclosures. The 
framework does not show a broad negative workforce signal attributable to 
AI. The aggregate score remains close to the level of no impact on the labour 
force across all of our sample. However, this does not rule out AI displacing 
workers at a future date. 
— Geographic differences persist within the listed-company sample. Since 
2022, German companies in the STOXX 600 have generally recorded above-
average AI-use and reported productivity scores. Dutch and Spanish 
companies in the STOXX 600 have tended to score lower on AI use, although 
Spain has recently gone through a rapid catch up. 
— The technology J-Curve: The findings are consistent with an early stage of 
the AI transition. Companies report increasing adoption of the new 
technology, but transformational productivity gains and extensive workforce 
restructuring remain uncommon.

1 1 Our US team regularly publish on data from the USCB showing the impact of 
AI on businesses, and our German team have recently begun their own 
chartbook. In the UK, the Office for National Statistics publishes on the topic too.

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

Methodology 
Sample period: Our sample begins in January 2022, providing a baseline before 
ChatGPT’s launch on 30 November 2022. AI in this framework covers both 
established and emerging technologies, so the launch of ChatGPT is a reference 
point rather than the start of all corporate AI adoption. 
Timing of observations: STOXX 600 companies report on different financial 
calendars and at quarterly or semi-annual intervals. Their earnings calls also mix 
discussion of completed activity with forward-looking guidance. 
We assign each transcript to the calendar quarter in which the earnings call takes 
place, regardless of the financial reporting period discussed. The resulting 
indicators therefore track when information about AI use and its operational 
impacts becomes public rather than when implementation or the reported effect 
occurred. This provides a common dating convention across the sample while 
retaining differences in reporting frequency and disclosure timing.

Figure 1: Coverage of the STOXX 600 earnings calls

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: Share of the STOXX 600 is based on the current company weight in the index. Data collection for the current quarter (Q4 2026) is incomplete 
as some companies are still to report

Constructing the STOXX 600 AI corpus: We use a specialised AI transcription 
engine to process earnings calls from STOXX 600 companies, restricting the 
analysis to calls for which we obtain complete transcripts of sufficient accuracy. 
In Q3 2026, the latest full quarter, the dataset covered more than 500 companies, 
representing approximately 90% of the index’s current market capitalisation 
weights (Figure 1). In the current quarter (Q4) earnings calls are still taking place. 
Nearly 250 companies with an equivalent weight to around c.60% of the STOXX 
index having already reported. 
Historical coverage typically ranges from 300 to 500 companies and represents 
more than 80% of the index’s current market-capitalisation weights. Coverage 
peaks twice a year because some European companies report semi-annually.  
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 3

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3

### Page 4

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

For examples of the AI’s scoring and justifications, see the tables presented in the 
Annex at the end of this report. 
Summary statistics 
Figure 2 shows the distribution of scores across all earnings-call transcripts in the 
historical sample, without weighting for company size or reporting frequency. 
These are transcript-level distributions rather than shares of companies, and they 
combine early and recent observations. They provide context for the quarterly 
indicators: 
• AI use score: Approximately 60% of earnings call transcripts do not 
discuss AI use. Among the minority that do discuss it, most fall into the 
“Active implementation” range (a score of 4-6 out of 10). 
• AI’s productivity impact: Nearly 70% of earnings calls do not mention a 
tangible AI-related productivity benefit. Among calls that do, scores are 
generally in the medium-impact band, indicating notable operational 
improvements. Transformational gains, scored at 8 or above out of 10, 
are rarely reported. 
• AI’s labour impact: Only around 10% of earnings calls discuss AI-related 
workforce adjustments. Reported changes typically receive scores of 1–
2 in magnitude on the −5 to +5 scale, with a slight tilt towards hiring and 
upskilling rather than redundancies.

Figure 2: Raw distribution of scores across the entire sample

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Note: Results are based on the raw number of transcripts, and are independent of firm size, and biased towards firms reporting more often.

Taken together, the distributions are consistent with an early adoption phase: 
companies discuss AI implementation more often than transformational 
productivity gains or extensive workforce restructuring. A technology J-curve 
offers one interpretation of this pattern, with investment and organisational 
adaptation preceding broader benefits.  
This J-curve pattern is strongly corroborated by external micro-data, such as the 
Banco de España’s business activity survey (EBAE) of Spanish firms, which 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 5

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

similarly indicates that AI has a more immediate impact on upfront investment 
and productivity than on headcount. 
AI use: A broad-based rise, with signs of faster recent adoption 
The new AI-use indicator points to a broadening transition across STOXX 600 
companies, with early signs that adoption has accelerated recently. Scores rose 
only gradually from 2022 and 2024, but the latest observations suggest a 
stronger pace across industries and country groups.  
The rise is broad-based rather than confined to one sector. Technology has led 
much of the sample, while financials show tentative recent outperformance and 
utilities have caught up from a low starting point. Among the large country groups, 
German companies have persistently recorded above-average scores.  
AI use across European industries 
To trace sectoral dispersion, we group companies by STOXX industry 
classification and calculate a weighted average of their scores for each calendar 
quarter, using their latest STOXX 600 index weights. 2  
We focus on four sectors spanning different business models: financials, the 
largest services sector; industrials, the largest physical-assets sector; technology, 
a likely early adopter; and utilities, an infrastructure-heavy sector where adoption 
may proceed more slowly.  
Figure 3 presents the AI use score for these four key industries alongside the 
range for all industry scores (represented by the shaded area). 
• The AI use score is generally low over time but is gradually rising from 
around 1 point in 2022 to around 3 in 2026. 
• AI-use scores are rising across industries, but differences between 
sectors are widening. The common direction therefore points to 
broadening adoption rather than uniform progress: sectors can 
participate in the transition while remaining at different levels of use. 
In terms of the industries of interest: 
• Technology’s early lead has narrowed. The sector’s AI-use score was 
near the top of the industry range through to 2024 but has recently 
plateaued as the broader STOXX 600 average caught up during 2025–
26.3 
• Financials and industrials broadly track the index average. Both sectors 
have remained close to the aggregate AI-use score over history, with 
financials generally a little below average, and industrials a little above. 
But financials show tentative signs of moving above average in recent 
quarters.

2 The industries are: (1) Basic Materials, (2), Consumer Discretionary, (3), Consumer Staples, (4) Energy, (5) 
Financials, (6) Health Care, (7) Industrials, (9) Real Estate, (10) Technology, (11) Telecommunications, (12) 
Utilities. 
3 Q4 2025 saw a large increase in the AI use score for the “Technology” industry, jumping up to around 6. This 
one-off spike was driven by ASML’s announcement of a significant strategic partnership with and investment 
in Mistral AI” to 'embed AI across our entire holistic portfolio in order to increase the performance and 
productivity of our systems and the yield of our customers' processes'. Because of ASML’s large market cap 
(around 49% of our Technology category), it distorts the weighted average significantly.

• Utilities have caught up from a low base. After recording lower AI-use 
scores than other sectors for much of the sample, utilities converged 
towards the STOXX 600 average during 2026. The result shows that the 
rise in reported adoption extends beyond the sectors most closely 
associated with AI.

Figure 3: AI use score (0-10) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The value for each industry is computed as a weighted average of all firms within the industry where the weights are derived from the current 
STOXX 600 share of each company. The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

As a cross-check, our findings align with the European Commission’s October 
Eurobarometer Flash survey of MSEs and Mid-Caps and the Eurostat annual ICT 
surveys, which show low overall intensity but a similar expansion in service- and 
tech-related sectors.  
AI use across countries 
Companies in the STOXX 600 tend to be large multi-nationals. That makes it 
difficult to pin down where physically the AI transformation is taking place within 
their organisation. Instead, our country comparison is done using two 
classifications of a company’s country: 
• Country of domicile: AI is a transformation technology, and this type of 
change is likely to be driven in organisation from the top down. We 
therefore consider a company’s country as that in which it is domiciled 
(which is likely to be where the headquarters is also). 
• Country of primary listing: While companies can generally list in other 
countries, there tends to be a home bias in their choice of exchange.  
Figure 4 presents the AI use intensity metric by both of these measures of country 
(the company’s domicile and primary exchange listing). Again, the shaded area 
shows the range across all classifications, with the largest countries/exchanges 
singled out. 
German-domiciled companies and the Xetra-listed (Frankfurt stock exchange) 
group record above-average AI-use scores in most quarters since 2022.  
Spain’s AI-use score has risen noticeably over recent quarters and now exceeds 
the aggregate average, reversing its earlier below-average position.

AI-use scores rise across country groups, with indications of faster growth from 
2025. The shared direction suggests a broad European listed-company trend, 
even though differences in score levels and timing persist between countries.

As a geographical cross-check, our index matches the broad trends of the 
Eurostat ICT annual survey, which places Germany as a clear frontrunner among 
its large peers. But some differences do exist. For instance, Eurostat shows the 
Netherlands with a very high AI adoption. As Figure 5 demonstrates, there is a 
positive correlation between the AI use score metric derived here, and that from 
Eurostat, even though the Eurostat data measures if firms are adopting AI, and 
our new use measure also incorporates the concept of how intensively firms are 
using AI.

AI productivity impact: Reported benefits are rising, but 
transformational gains remain uncommon 
Higher intensity of use does not necessarily mean firms are benefiting more from 
AI. But our AI productivity impact metric does show a similar pattern to our AI 
usage metric, although it tends to be slightly lower, indicating that companies 
may not yet be reaping the full rewards for their AI investment. 
Financials stand out more clearly on reported productivity benefits than on AI-use 
intensity, while German companies again record relatively strong scores, but only 
in more recent history.

Figure 6: AI's impact on productivity (0-10) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

AI productivity impact across European industries 
Reported AI-productivity benefits have become more prominent across 
industries. Figure 6 shows scores rising gradually during 2022–25, followed by 
11 November 2026

indications of a faster increase from late 2025. The profile resembles the AI-use 
trend in Figure 4, suggesting that the stronger adoption narrative is accompanied 
by more discussion of operational gains. 
The aggregate trajectories of the AI use and AI productivity impact scores are 
similar, but sector rankings differ. Technology’s early relative strength in AI use is 
not matched by its productivity score, while financials report above-average 
productivity benefits despite more modest use scores. 
AI productivity impact across countries 
Figure 7 plots the self-report impact of AI on company productivity by country. 
Between 2022-23, German companies recorded average productivity impact, 
even though their AI use score was relatively high. However more recently, their 
AI impact score has persistently been above average. The UK grouping shows 
persistent early outperformance in AI impact during 2022–23, followed by 
convergence towards the aggregate average.

AI labour force impact: On net, AI may be benefiting 
employment, for now… 
Across industries and countries, the recorded score for AI’s impact on the labour 
force remains relatively small. On average, the reported impact on companies’ 
employment has been fairly neutral, with a marginal negative bias over the last 
year or so.

Figure 9: AI's impact on the labour force (-5 to +5) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter. Negative (positive) scores are associated with job losses (gains) 
associated with a company's AI use.

11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 11

Deutsche Bank AG Page 11

AI labour force impact across European industries 
Despite rising productivity, AI has not triggered the negative employment shock 
feared by the public. Aggregate labour impact scores across nearly all sectors 
hover near zero, with a slightly positive balance over history, and a slightly 
negative balance more recently in 2025-26 (Figure 9). But overall the magnitude 
of AI’s impact on labour remains low. For the STOXX 600 aggregate average, the 
score’s maximum deviation from 0 (no impact) is 0.43 on our 0-5 scale. 
AI labour force impact across countries 
Workforce-impact scores are also small across the main country groupings, with 
most countries slowing a slight negative tilt in recent years (Figure 10).

Figure 10: AI impact on the labour force (-5 to +5) by country

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter. Negative (positive) scores are associated with job losses (gains) 
associated with a company's AI use.

The lack of significant impact so far from AI on companies’ labour forces so far is 
consistent with results from our previous work looking at the other side of the 
labour market. Data from our dbDataInsights household survey showed that 
workers across Europe reported a fairly low level of fear about losing their job to 
AI over the next year. The analysis in that report found results that were more 
consistent with workers adapting to the new AI world by upskilling and 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 12

Deutsche Bank AG Page 12

demanding higher wages. Those results would not be consistent with a world in 
which AI was actively replacing labour. 
These findings of a low impact on labour are highly consistent with recent 
academic research (e.g., Yotzov et al., 2026), which found that nearly half of 
surveyed UK and German firms reported absolutely no impact on headcount from 
AI over the past three years, 
Conclusion 
The newly developed framework provides a recurring way to monitor company 
disclosures about AI’s impact on productivity and labour. The central finding of 
this report is a broadening AI transition rather than clear evidence of an 
economy-wide productivity breakthrough. The new metrics indicate that AI use 
remains limited but is rising across the European countries and industries 
represented in the sample. Companies report low but growing productivity gains 
and little aggregate workforce impact so far. This pattern is consistent with a 
technology J-curve, with firms still integrating the new technology, rather than 
proof that this trajectory will unfold. Limited reported workforce effects today do 
not rule out subsequent layoffs or wider organisational restructuring. 
Quarterly updates to this framework will track Europe’s AI transition and provide 
timely signals of whether companies are reporting more substantial productivity 
and employment effects. 
Beyond AI, the framework could be adapted to extract signals about employment 
decisions, investment decisions and the general economic outlook from earnings 
calls. These potential extensions could form the basis of alternative nowcasting 
models for important macroeconomic data; they are applications that will be 
explored in future work.

11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

Deutsche Bank AG Page 13
